then FULL_PARAM=\"--full\"; fi\n124\tGSD_WS=$(echo \" $ARGUMENTS\" | sed -nE 's/.* --ws +([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*).*/--ws \\1/p' | head -n 1)\n125\tINIT=$(gsd_run query init.quick ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }} \"$DESCRIPTION\" $DISCUSS_PARAM $RESEARCH_PARAM $VALIDATE_PARAM $FULL_PARAM)\n126\tif [[ \"$INIT\" == @file:* ]]; then INIT=$(cat \"${INIT#@file:}\"); fi\n127\tAGENT_SKILLS_PLANNER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-planner ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n128\tAGENT_SKILLS_EXECUTOR=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-executor ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n129\tAGENT_SKILLS_CHECKER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-plan-checker ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n130\tAGENT_SKILLS_VERIFIER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-verifier ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n131\tAGENT_SKILLS_RESEARCHER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-phase-researcher ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n132\t```\n133\t\n134\tParse JSON for: `planner_model`, `executor_model`, `checker_model`, `verifier_model`, `reviewer_model`, `researcher_model`, `commit_docs`, `branch_name`, `quick_id`, `slug`, `date`, `timestamp`, `quick_dir`, `task_dir`, `roadmap_exists`, `planning_exists`, `response_language`.\n135\t\n136\t`init.quick` does not emit dedicated `state_path`/`project_path` fields, so derive them from the already-absolute `quick_dir` (#2376 — files handed to a spawned subagent must resolve regardless of that subagent's own cwd):\n137\t```bash\n138\tSTATE_PATH=\"$(dirname \"${quick_dir}\")/STATE.md\"\n139\tPROJECT_PATH=\"$(dirname \"${quick_dir}\")/PROJECT.md\"\n140\t```\n141\t\n142\t```bash\n143\tUSE_WORKTREES=$(gsd_run query config-get workflow.use_worktrees --raw 2>/dev/null || echo \"true\")\n144\tRUNTIME=$(gsd_run query config-get runtime --default claude --raw 2>/dev/null || echo \"claude\")\n145\t```\n146\t\n147\t**Resolve isolation now (#2584/#2652).** Read @gsd-core/references/dispatch-isolation-gate.md\n148\tand run its `Resolve ISOLATION`, `Single-agent dispatch sites`, and `Resolve the harness flag`\n149\tblocks in order; they set `ISOLATION`/`HARNESS_FLAG` via `query dispatch-isolation`.\n150\t`ISOLATION` — not `RUNTIME` — gates every worktree decision below. Substitute `{harnessFlag}`\n151\tin Step 6's `Agent()` with `$HARNESS_FLAG`+comma when `ISOLATION = \"harness-worktree\"`, else\n152\tempty. `{harnessFlag}`\n153\tis a template placeholder, not a shell variable.\n154\t\n155\tIf `USE_WORKTREES` is not `\"false\"`, run a startup orphan sweep before spawning any executors. This reaps locked worktrees whose lock-owner process is dead, whose branch is merged into the default branch, and whose lock file mtime is older than 5 minutes. Running it at startup prevents accumulation of orphaned worktrees from prior sessions that exited without cleanup (#3707).\n156\t\n157\t```bash\n158\tif [ \"$USE_WORKTREES\" != \"false\" ]; then\n159\t  gsd_run query worktree.reap-orphans 2>/dev/null || true\n160\tfi\n161\t```\n162\t\n163\tIf the project uses git submodules, worktree isolation is unsafe **only when the quick task touches a submodule path**. The previous behavior unconditionally disabled worktree isolation whenever `.gitmodules` existed, which penalised every quick task in a submodule project even when the task was nowhere near a submodule. Parse submodule paths from `.gitmodules` so the executor can act on actual submodule paths rather than the mere file's existence:\n164\t\n165\t```bash\n166\t# Parse submodule paths from .gitmodules once (empty if no .gitmodules).\n167\t# SUBMODULE_PATHS is a newline-separated list of repo-relative paths used as\n168\t# a fail-loud commit-time guard inside the quick-task executor — if the\n169\t# executor stages any path that falls inside SUBMODULE_PATHS, it must abort\n170\t# the commit and surface the conflict rather than silently corrupting the\n171\t# submodule state.\n172\tif [ -f .gitmodules ]; then\n173\t  SUBMODULE_PATHS=$(git config --file .gitmodules --get-regexp '^submodule\\..*\\.path$' 2>/dev/null | awk '{print $2}')\n174\telse\n175\t  SUBMODULE_PATHS=\"\"\n176\tfi\n177\t```\n178\t\n179\tQuick mode does not have a pre-declared `files_modified` list (the task is freeform), so use a fail-loud guard at commit time: when the executor stages files for the quick-task commit, if any staged path falls inside a `SUBMODULE_PATHS` entry, abort with a clear error explaining that worktree-isolated commits cannot safely span submodule boundaries — the user can re-run with `workflow.use_worktrees=false` to fall back to sequential execution on the main tree. If `SUBMODULE_PATHS` is empty (no `.gitmodules` in the repo), worktree isolation proceeds normally.\n180\t\n181\t**If `roadmap_exists` is false:** Error — Quick mode requires an active project with ROADMAP.md. Run `/gsd-new-project` first.\n182\t\n183\tQuick tasks can run mid-phase - validation only checks ROADMAP.md exists, not phase status.\n184\t\n185\t---\n186\t\n187\t**Step 2.5: Handle quick-task branching**\n188\t\n189\t**If `branch_name` is empty/null:** Skip and continue on the current branch.\n190\t\n191\t**If `branch_name` is set:** Check out the quick-task branch before any planning commits.\n192\t\n193\tThe new branch must fork off the project's default branch (`origin/HEAD`), not\n194\toff whatever HEAD happens to be checked out — otherwise consecutive quick tasks\n195\tcompound on top of each other and stay unpushed (#2916). If `$branch_name`\n196\talready exists locally, reuse it as-is so resumed work is not rebased.\n197\t\n198\t```bash\n199\tDEFAULT_BRANCH=$(gsd_run query git.base-branch 2>/dev/null \\\n200\t  || git symbolic-ref --quiet --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD 2>/dev/null | sed 's|^origin/||' \\\n201\t  || echo main)\n202\t\n203\tif git show-ref --verify --quiet \"refs/heads/$branch_name\"; then\n204\t  git switch \"$branch_name\" \\\n205\t    || { echo \"ERROR: Could not switch to existing quick-task branch '$branch_name'.\" >&2; exit 1; }\n206\telse\n207\t  # Fetch the default branch so origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH is current. If the fetch\n208\t  # fails (offline, no remote, auth failure) AND we have no local copy of\n209\t  # origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH to fall back on, abort — creating the branch off\n210\t  # arbitrary HEAD is exactly the bug #2916 fixed.\n211\t  if ! git fetch --quiet origin \"$DEFAULT_BRANCH\"; then\n212\t    if ! git show-ref --verify --quiet \"refs/remotes/origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\"; then\n213\t      echo \"ERROR: Could not fetch origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH and no local copy exists. Refusing to create '$branch_name' off the current HEAD (#2916). Resolve the remote/network issue and retry.\" >&2\n214\t      exit 1\n215\t    fi\n216\t    echo \"WARNING: git fetch origin $DEFAULT_BRANCH failed; using the local copy of origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH as base.\" >&2\n217\t  fi\n218\t\n219\t  if [ -n \"$(git status --porcelain)\" ]; then\n220\t    echo \"WARNING: Uncommitted changes present. Carrying them onto the new quick-task branch — they will be branched off origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH (not the previous-task HEAD).\"\n221\t  else\n222\t    # Best-effort: fast-forward the local default branch so subsequent local\n223\t    # work sees the latest tip. Failure here is non-fatal because we always\n224\t    # create the new branch directly from origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH below.\n225\t    git switch --quiet \"$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" 2>/dev/null \\\n226\t      && git merge --ff-only --quiet \"origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" 2>/dev/null \\\n227\t      || true\n228\t  fi\n229\t\n230\t  # Pin the new branch to origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH so the start point is\n231\t  # deterministic regardless of which branch we are currently on (#2916).\n232\t  # On success HEAD is exactly at origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH, so a post-creation\n233\t  # merge-base / \"ahead-of\" guard would be unreachable — the explicit base\n234\t  # argument here is the single source of correctness for #2916.\n235\t  # --no-track: with the default branch.autoSetupMerge=true, checkout -b from a\n236\t  # remote-tracking ref wires branch.<name>.merge to refs/heads/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\n237\t  # (origin/master), so a GUI sync pushes quick-task commits straight onto\n238\t  # origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH, bypassing PR review (#2498).\n239\t  git checkout -b \"$branch_name\" \"origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" --no-track \\\n240\t    || { echo \"ERROR: Could not create '$branch_name' from origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH (#2916).\" >&2; exit 1; }\n241\tfi\n242\t```\n243\t\n244\tAll quick-task commits for this run stay on that branch. User handles merge/rebase afterward.\n245\t\n246\t---\n247\t\n248\t**Step 3: Create task directory**\n249\t\n250\t```bash\n251\tmkdir -p \"${task_dir}\"\n252\t```\n253\t\n254\t---\n255\t\n256\t**Step 4: Create quick task directory**\n257\t\n258\tCreate the directory for this quick task:\n259\t\n260\t```bash\n261\tQUICK_DIR=\"${task_dir}\"\n262\tmkdir -p \"$QUICK_DIR\"\n263\t```\n264\t\n265\tReport to user:\n266\t```\n267\tCreating quick task ${quick_id}: ${DESCRIPTION}\n268\tDirectory: ${QUICK_DIR}\n269\t```\n270\t\n271\tStore `$QUICK_DIR` for use in orchestration.\n272\t\n273\t---\n274\t\n275\t**Step 4 ordering (#3894):** Check whether `workflow.research_before_questions` is enabled in `.planning/config.json` (or the config from init context) — the same check `/gsd-discuss-phase` and `/gsd-new-project` already make. When **enabled**, execute the research-phase section BELOW BEFORE the discussion-phase section: a gray-area answer given without research is written to `<quick_id>-CONTEXT.md` as a locked decision downstream agents are told not to revisit, so the evidence must come first. When **false or unset**, keep the written order (discussion, then research) — behavior unchanged.\n276\t\n277\tIf `section_manifest` is `null` or `\"discussion-phase\"` is in its `included` list: read and execute `gsd-core/workflows/quick/steps/discussion-phase.md`. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n278\t\n279\t---\n280\t\n281\tIf `section_manifest` is `null` or `\"research-phase\"` is in its `included` list: read and execute `gsd-core/workflows/quick/steps/research-phase.md`. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n282\t\n283\t---\n284\t\n285\t**Step 5: Spawn planner (quick mode)**\n286\t\n287\t**Capability gate:**\n288\t```bash\n289\tPLAN_PRE_HOOKS_JSON=$(gsd_run loop render-hooks plan:pre --raw)\n290\t```\n291\t\n292\t**Contribution dispatch (#3778):** read `PLAN_PRE_HOOKS_JSON.activeHooks` directly in context. Reuse this snapshot for revisions; do not render again.\n293\t\n294\t**If `$VALIDATE_MODE`:** Use `quick-full` mode with stricter constraints.\n295\t\n296\t**If NOT `$VALIDATE_MODE`:** Use standard `quick` mode.\n297\t\n298\tDisplay: `◆ Spawning planner... (runs in a subagent — no output until it returns, ~1–5 min; expected, not a freeze)`\n299\t\n300\t```\n301\tAgent(\n302\t  prompt=\"\n303\t<planning_context>\n304\t\n305\t**Mode:** ${VALIDATE_MODE ? This prevents duplicate work, conflicting edits, and wasted context. Only resume when the subagent result is available.\n347\t\n348\tAfter planner returns:\n349\t1. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n362\t\n363\t---\n364\t\n365\t**Step 6: Spawn executor**\n366\t\n367\tAuto-degrade to sequential if HEAD has diverged from the worktree fork base (#1941, mirrors\n368\texecute-phase's #683/#1369 guard). Claude Code's `isolation=\"worktree\"` forks new worktrees from\n369\t`origin/HEAD`, not the live local HEAD. If a prior quick task in this session (or the Step 5.6\n370\tpre-dispatch plan commit above) advanced local HEAD without an intervening `git push`,\n371\t`origin/HEAD` stays pinned to a stale ancestor and the executor's `worktree_branch_check` guard\n372\thalts with a base-mismatch fatal — potentially many commits behind, not just one. Run this check\n373\timmediately before capturing `EXPECTED_BASE` so it reflects the most current local state.\n374\t\n375\t```bash\n376\tif [ \"$ISOLATION\" = \"harness-worktree\" ] && [ \"${USE_WORKTREES:-true}\" != \"false\" ]; then\n377\t  _QUICK_SHOULD_DEGRADE=$(gsd_run query worktree.base-check --mode \"$ISOLATION\" --pick shouldDegrade 2>/dev/null || true)\n378\t  if [ \"$_QUICK_SHOULD_DEGRADE\" = \"true\" ]; then\n379\t    _QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG=$(gsd_run query worktree.base-check --mode \"$ISOLATION\" --pick message 2>/dev/null || true)\n380\t    [ -n \"$_QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG\" ] && printf '%s\\n' \"$_QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG\" >&2\n381\t    echo \"⚠ [#1941] Worktree fork base diverged from orchestrator HEAD — auto-degrading to sequential mode for this quick task to avoid a base-mismatch halt.\" >&2\n382\t    USE_WORKTREES=false\n383\t    ISOLATION=none\n384\t  fi\n385\tfi\n386\t\n387\t# Re-resolve (and, as a side effect, re-persist) now that the base-check\n388\t# auto-degrade above may have changed $ISOLATION since the Step 2 gate's\n389\t# `dispatch-isolation` call (#3045). That first call recorded the NATURALLY\n390\t# resolved mode into the run-scoped sentinel the isolation guard hooks read\n391\t# (hooks/gsd-agent-isolation-guard.js, hooks/gsd-cursor-subagent-start.js via\n392\t# hooks/lib/isolation-sentinel.js). The degrade above is decided HERE, in\n393\t# shell — the resolver cannot see it — so without this the sentinel still\n394\t# asserts `harness-worktree` while the dispatch below correctly omits the\n395\t# harness flag, and the guard denies the dispatch with exit 2. `--force-isolation`\n396\t# pushes the FINAL, shell-computed value through that SAME single write path\n397\t# (`none` also clears the stored harnessFlag, since none applies to sequential\n398\t# dispatch). Best-effort: a write failure here must never fail the task — the\n399\t# guards' own sentinel-absent fallback is safe, just less precise.\n400\tgsd_run query dispatch-isolation --raw --force-isolation \"$ISOLATION\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true\n401\t```\n402\t\n403\tCapture current HEAD before spawning (used for worktree branch check):\n404\t```bash\n405\tEXPECTED_BASE=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n406\tif [ \"$ISOLATION\" = \"harness-worktree\" ]; then   # keyed on ISOLATION like every other dispatch-coupled branch (#2652)\n407\t  # BSD/macOS mktemp only randomizes XXXXXX when it is the final path component, so make a\n408\t  # suffixless temp then append the extension — portable across BSD + GNU (#1520).\n409\t  QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST=$(mktemp \"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/gsd-quick-worktree-XXXXXX\") && mv \"$QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\" \"${QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST}.json\" && QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST=\"${QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST}.json\" || exit 1\n410\t  printf '{\"worktrees\":[]}\\n' > \"$QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\"\n411\t  export QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\n412\tfi\n413\t```\n414\t\n415\tSpawn gsd-executor with plan reference:\n416\t\n417\t```\n418\tAgent(\n419\t  prompt=\"\n420\tExecute quick task ${quick_id}.\n421\t\n422\t${ISOLATION === \"harness-worktree\" ? `\n423\t<worktree_branch_check>\n424\tORCHESTRATOR build-time embed (NOT a sub-agent runtime step): before this dispatch, read \\`gsd-core/references/worktree-branch-check.md\\`, substitute \\`{EXPECTED_BASE}\\` with the base SHA captured above (${EXPECTED_BASE}), substitute \\`{EXPECTED_BASE_ALTERNATE}\\` with \\`${QUICK_PLAN_PARENT}\\` when it differs from \\`${EXPECTED_BASE}\\` (otherwise empty), and replace this note with that fragment's \\`<worktree_branch_check>\\` block so the dispatched prompt carries the runnable guard verbatim — do not pass this instruction through in its place.\n425\t</worktree_branch_check>\n426\t\n427\tFIRST ACTION after the worktree branch check: ensure the quick PLAN.md exists at a worktree-rooted relative path before any Read/Edit/Write path can be primed. If \\`${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md\\` is absent, materialize it from the shared git object store:\n428\t\n429\t\\`\\`\\`bash\n430\tQUICK_PLAN_COMMIT=\"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}\"\n431\tQUICK_PLAN_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md\"\n432\tif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\" ]; then\n433\t  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\")\"\n434\t  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:${QUICK_PLAN_PATH}\" > \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\" || {\n435\t    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick plan from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:${QUICK_PLAN_PATH}; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n436\t    exit 42\n437\t  }\n438\tfi\n439\t${DISCUSS_MODE ? `QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-CONTEXT.md\"\n440\tif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" ] && git cat-file -e \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" 2>/dev/null; then\n441\t  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\")\"\n442\t  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" > \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" || {\n443\t    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick context from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n444\t    exit 42\n445\t  }\n446\tfi\n447\t` : ''}${RESEARCH_MODE ? `QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-RESEARCH.md\"\n448\tif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" ] && git cat-file -e \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" 2>/dev/null; then\n449\t  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\")\"\n450\t  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" > \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" || {\n451\t    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick research from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n452\t    exit 42\n453\t  }\n454\tfi\n455\t` : ''}\\`\\`\\`\n456\t` : ''}\n457\t\n458\t<required_reading>\n459\t- ${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md (Plan)\n460\t${DISCUSS_MODE ? Investigates implementation approaches, library options, and pitfalls. See docs/how-to/diagnose-a-foreign-gsd-tools.md\" >&2; if [ -n \"${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE:-}\" ] && [ -n \"${GSD_TOOLS:-}\" ]; then printf \"export PATH='%s':\\\"\\$PATH\\\"\\n\" \"${GSD_TOOLS%/*}\" >> \"$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true; fi\nRESPONSE_LANGUAGE=$(gsd_run query config-get response_language --raw --default \"\" 2>/dev/null || echo \"\")\n```\n\n**If `response_language` is set:** All user-facing output of this workflow — narration between tool calls, status updates, progress notes, findings, questions, prompts, and explanations — MUST be presented in `{response_language}`. Technical terms, code, file paths, and subagent prompts stay in English — only user-facing output is translated.\n\nIf `$DESCRIPTION` is empty after parsing, prompt user interactively:\n\n**Text mode (`workflow.text_mode: true` in config or `--text` flag):** Set `TEXT_MODE=true` if `--text` is present in `$ARGUMENTS` OR `text_mode` from init JSON is `true`. When TEXT_MODE is active, replace every `AskUserQuestion` call with a plain-text numbered list and ask the user to type their choice number. This is required for non-Claude runtimes (OpenAI Codex, Antigravity, etc.) where `AskUserQuestion` is not available.\n\n```\nAskUserQuestion(\n  header: \"Quick Task\",\n  question: \"What do you want to do?\",\n  followUp: null\n)\n```\n\nStore response as `$DESCRIPTION`.\n\nIf still empty, re-prompt: \"Please provide a task description.\"\n\nDisplay banner based on active flags:\n\nIf `$FULL_MODE` (all phases enabled — `--full` or all granular flags):\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (FULL)\n\n◆ Discussion + research + plan checking + verification enabled\n```\n\nIf `$DISCUSS_MODE` and `$VALIDATE_MODE` (no research):\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (DISCUSS + VALIDATE)\n\n◆ Discussion + plan checking + verification enabled\n```\n\nIf `$DISCUSS_MODE` and `$RESEARCH_MODE` (no validate):\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (DISCUSS + RESEARCH)\n\n◆ Discussion + research enabled\n```\n\nIf `$RESEARCH_MODE` and `$VALIDATE_MODE` (no discuss):\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (RESEARCH + VALIDATE)\n\n◆ Research + plan checking + verification enabled\n```\n\nIf `$DISCUSS_MODE` only:\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (DISCUSS)\n\n◆ Discussion phase enabled — surfacing gray areas before planning\n```\n\nIf `$RESEARCH_MODE` only:\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (RESEARCH)\n\n◆ Research phase enabled — investigating approaches before planning\n```\n\nIf `$VALIDATE_MODE` only:\n```\n### GSD ► QUICK TASK (VALIDATE)\n\n◆ Plan checking + verification enabled\n```\n\n---\n\n**Step 2: Initialize**\n\n```bash\nDISCUSS_PARAM=\"\"; if [[ \"$ARGUMENTS\" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--discuss([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then DISCUSS_PARAM=\"--discuss\"; fi\nRESEARCH_PARAM=\"\"; if [[ \"$ARGUMENTS\" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--research([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then RESEARCH_PARAM=\"--research\"; fi\nVALIDATE_PARAM=\"\"; if [[ \"$ARGUMENTS\" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--validate([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then VALIDATE_PARAM=\"--validate\"; fi\nFULL_PARAM=\"\"; if [[ \"$ARGUMENTS\" =~ (^|[[:space:]])--full([[:space:]]|$) ]]; then FULL_PARAM=\"--full\"; fi\nGSD_WS=$(echo \" $ARGUMENTS\" | sed -nE 's/.* --ws +([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*).*/--ws \\1/p' | head -n 1)\nINIT=$(gsd_run query init.quick ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }} \"$DESCRIPTION\" $DISCUSS_PARAM $RESEARCH_PARAM $VALIDATE_PARAM $FULL_PARAM)\nif [[ \"$INIT\" == @file:* ]]; then INIT=$(cat \"${INIT#@file:}\"); fi\nAGENT_SKILLS_PLANNER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-planner ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\nAGENT_SKILLS_EXECUTOR=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-executor ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\nAGENT_SKILLS_CHECKER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-plan-checker ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\nAGENT_SKILLS_VERIFIER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-verifier ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\nAGENT_SKILLS_RESEARCHER=$(gsd_run query agent-skills gsd-phase-researcher ${GSD_WS:+--ws=${GSD_WS##* }})\n```\n\nParse JSON for: `planner_model`, `executor_model`, `checker_model`, `verifier_model`, `reviewer_model`, `researcher_model`, `commit_docs`, `branch_name`, `quick_id`, `slug`, `date`, `timestamp`, `quick_dir`, `task_dir`, `roadmap_exists`, `planning_exists`, `response_language`.\n\n`init.quick` does not emit dedicated `state_path`/`project_path` fields, so derive them from the already-absolute `quick_dir` (#2376 — files handed to a spawned subagent must resolve regardless of that subagent's own cwd):\n```bash\nSTATE_PATH=\"$(dirname \"${quick_dir}\")/STATE.md\"\nPROJECT_PATH=\"$(dirname \"${quick_dir}\")/PROJECT.md\"\n```\n\n```bash\nUSE_WORKTREES=$(gsd_run query config-get workflow.use_worktrees --raw 2>/dev/null || echo \"true\")\nRUNTIME=$(gsd_run query config-get runtime --default claude --raw 2>/dev/null || echo \"claude\")\n```\n\n**Resolve isolation now (#2584/#2652).** Read @gsd-core/references/dispatch-isolation-gate.md\nand run its `Resolve ISOLATION`, `Single-agent dispatch sites`, and `Resolve the harness flag`\nblocks in order; they set `ISOLATION`/`HARNESS_FLAG` via `query dispatch-isolation`.\n`ISOLATION` — not `RUNTIME` — gates every worktree decision below. Substitute `{harnessFlag}`\nin Step 6's `Agent()` with `$HARNESS_FLAG`+comma when `ISOLATION = \"harness-worktree\"`, else\nempty. `{harnessFlag}`\nis a template placeholder, not a shell variable.\n\nIf `USE_WORKTREES` is not `\"false\"`, run a startup orphan sweep before spawning any executors. This reaps locked worktrees whose lock-owner process is dead, whose branch is merged into the default branch, and whose lock file mtime is older than 5 minutes. Running it at startup prevents accumulation of orphaned worktrees from prior sessions that exited without cleanup (#3707).\n\n```bash\nif [ \"$USE_WORKTREES\" != \"false\" ]; then\n  gsd_run query worktree.reap-orphans 2>/dev/null || true\nfi\n```\n\nIf the project uses git submodules, worktree isolation is unsafe **only when the quick task touches a submodule path**. The previous behavior unconditionally disabled worktree isolation whenever `.gitmodules` existed, which penalised every quick task in a submodule project even when the task was nowhere near a submodule. Parse submodule paths from `.gitmodules` so the executor can act on actual submodule paths rather than the mere file's existence:\n\n```bash\n# Parse submodule paths from .gitmodules once (empty if no .gitmodules).\n# SUBMODULE_PATHS is a newline-separated list of repo-relative paths used as\n# a fail-loud commit-time guard inside the quick-task executor — if the\n# executor stages any path that falls inside SUBMODULE_PATHS, it must abort\n# the commit and surface the conflict rather than silently corrupting the\n# submodule state.\nif [ -f .gitmodules ]; then\n  SUBMODULE_PATHS=$(git config --file .gitmodules --get-regexp '^submodule\\..*\\.path$' 2>/dev/null | awk '{print $2}')\nelse\n  SUBMODULE_PATHS=\"\"\nfi\n```\n\nQuick mode does not have a pre-declared `files_modified` list (the task is freeform), so use a fail-loud guard at commit time: when the executor stages files for the quick-task commit, if any staged path falls inside a `SUBMODULE_PATHS` entry, abort with a clear error explaining that worktree-isolated commits cannot safely span submodule boundaries — the user can re-run with `workflow.use_worktrees=false` to fall back to sequential execution on the main tree. If `SUBMODULE_PATHS` is empty (no `.gitmodules` in the repo), worktree isolation proceeds normally.\n\n**If `roadmap_exists` is false:** Error — Quick mode requires an active project with ROADMAP.md. Run `/gsd-new-project` first.\n\nQuick tasks can run mid-phase - validation only checks ROADMAP.md exists, not phase status.\n\n---\n\n**Step 2.5: Handle quick-task branching**\n\n**If `branch_name` is empty/null:** Skip and continue on the current branch.\n\n**If `branch_name` is set:** Check out the quick-task branch before any planning commits.\n\nThe new branch must fork off the project's default branch (`origin/HEAD`), not\noff whatever HEAD happens to be checked out — otherwise consecutive quick tasks\ncompound on top of each other and stay unpushed (#2916). If `$branch_name`\nalready exists locally, reuse it as-is so resumed work is not rebased.\n\n```bash\nDEFAULT_BRANCH=$(gsd_run query git.base-branch 2>/dev/null \\\n  || git symbolic-ref --quiet --short refs/remotes/origin/HEAD 2>/dev/null | sed 's|^origin/||' \\\n  || echo main)\n\nif git show-ref --verify --quiet \"refs/heads/$branch_name\"; then\n  git switch \"$branch_name\" \\\n    || { echo \"ERROR: Could not switch to existing quick-task branch '$branch_name'.\" >&2; exit 1; }\nelse\n  # Fetch the default branch so origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH is current. If the fetch\n  # fails (offline, no remote, auth failure) AND we have no local copy of\n  # origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH to fall back on, abort — creating the branch off\n  # arbitrary HEAD is exactly the bug #2916 fixed.\n  if ! git fetch --quiet origin \"$DEFAULT_BRANCH\"; then\n    if ! git show-ref --verify --quiet \"refs/remotes/origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\"; then\n      echo \"ERROR: Could not fetch origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH and no local copy exists. Refusing to create '$branch_name' off the current HEAD (#2916). Resolve the remote/network issue and retry.\" >&2\n      exit 1\n    fi\n    echo \"WARNING: git fetch origin $DEFAULT_BRANCH failed; using the local copy of origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH as base.\" >&2\n  fi\n\n  if [ -n \"$(git status --porcelain)\" ]; then\n    echo \"WARNING: Uncommitted changes present. Carrying them onto the new quick-task branch — they will be branched off origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH (not the previous-task HEAD).\"\n  else\n    # Best-effort: fast-forward the local default branch so subsequent local\n    # work sees the latest tip. Failure here is non-fatal because we always\n    # create the new branch directly from origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH below.\n    git switch --quiet \"$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" 2>/dev/null \\\n      && git merge --ff-only --quiet \"origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" 2>/dev/null \\\n      || true\n  fi\n\n  # Pin the new branch to origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH so the start point is\n  # deterministic regardless of which branch we are currently on (#2916).\n  # On success HEAD is exactly at origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH, so a post-creation\n  # merge-base / \"ahead-of\" guard would be unreachable — the explicit base\n  # argument here is the single source of correctness for #2916.\n  # --no-track: with the default branch.autoSetupMerge=true, checkout -b from a\n  # remote-tracking ref wires branch.<name>.merge to refs/heads/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\n  # (origin/master), so a GUI sync pushes quick-task commits straight onto\n  # origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH, bypassing PR review (#2498).\n  git checkout -b \"$branch_name\" \"origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH\" --no-track \\\n    || { echo \"ERROR: Could not create '$branch_name' from origin/$DEFAULT_BRANCH (#2916).\" >&2; exit 1; }\nfi\n```\n\nAll quick-task commits for this run stay on that branch. User handles merge/rebase afterward.\n\n---\n\n**Step 3: Create task directory**\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p \"${task_dir}\"\n```\n\n---\n\n**Step 4: Create quick task directory**\n\nCreate the directory for this quick task:\n\n```bash\nQUICK_DIR=\"${task_dir}\"\nmkdir -p \"$QUICK_DIR\"\n```\n\nReport to user:\n```\nCreating quick task ${quick_id}: ${DESCRIPTION}\nDirectory: ${QUICK_DIR}\n```\n\nStore `$QUICK_DIR` for use in orchestration.\n\n---\n\n**Step 4 ordering (#3894):** Check whether `workflow.research_before_questions` is enabled in `.planning/config.json` (or the config from init context) — the same check `/gsd-discuss-phase` and `/gsd-new-project` already make. When **enabled**, execute the research-phase section BELOW BEFORE the discussion-phase section: a gray-area answer given without research is written to `<quick_id>-CONTEXT.md` as a locked decision downstream agents are told not to revisit, so the evidence must come first. When **false or unset**, keep the written order (discussion, then research) — behavior unchanged.\n\nIf `section_manifest` is `null` or `\"discussion-phase\"` is in its `included` list: read and execute `gsd-core/workflows/quick/steps/discussion-phase.md`. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n\n---\n\nIf `section_manifest` is `null` or `\"research-phase\"` is in its `included` list: read and execute `gsd-core/workflows/quick/steps/research-phase.md`. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n\n---\n\n**Step 5: Spawn planner (quick mode)**\n\n**Capability gate:**\n```bash\nPLAN_PRE_HOOKS_JSON=$(gsd_run loop render-hooks plan:pre --raw)\n```\n\n**Contribution dispatch (#3778):** read `PLAN_PRE_HOOKS_JSON.activeHooks` directly in context. Reuse this snapshot for revisions; do not render again.\n\n**If `$VALIDATE_MODE`:** Use `quick-full` mode with stricter constraints.\n\n**If NOT `$VALIDATE_MODE`:** Use standard `quick` mode.\n\nDisplay: `◆ Spawning planner... (runs in a subagent — no output until it returns, ~1–5 min; expected, not a freeze)`\n\n```\nAgent(\n  prompt=\"\n<planning_context>\n\n**Mode:** ${VALIDATE_MODE ? Do not read more files, edit code, or run tests related to this task while the subagent is active. Wait for the subagent to return its result. This prevents duplicate work, conflicting edits, and wasted context. Only resume when the subagent result is available.\n\nAfter planner returns:\n1. Otherwise skip — do not read the file.\n\n---\n\n**Step 6: Spawn executor**\n\nAuto-degrade to sequential if HEAD has diverged from the worktree fork base (#1941, mirrors\nexecute-phase's #683/#1369 guard). Claude Code's `isolation=\"worktree\"` forks new worktrees from\n`origin/HEAD`, not the live local HEAD. If a prior quick task in this session (or the Step 5.6\npre-dispatch plan commit above) advanced local HEAD without an intervening `git push`,\n`origin/HEAD` stays pinned to a stale ancestor and the executor's `worktree_branch_check` guard\nhalts with a base-mismatch fatal — potentially many commits behind, not just one. Run this check\nimmediately before capturing `EXPECTED_BASE` so it reflects the most current local state.\n\n```bash\nif [ \"$ISOLATION\" = \"harness-worktree\" ] && [ \"${USE_WORKTREES:-true}\" != \"false\" ]; then\n  _QUICK_SHOULD_DEGRADE=$(gsd_run query worktree.base-check --mode \"$ISOLATION\" --pick shouldDegrade 2>/dev/null || true)\n  if [ \"$_QUICK_SHOULD_DEGRADE\" = \"true\" ]; then\n    _QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG=$(gsd_run query worktree.base-check --mode \"$ISOLATION\" --pick message 2>/dev/null || true)\n    [ -n \"$_QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG\" ] && printf '%s\\n' \"$_QUICK_DEGRADE_MSG\" >&2\n    echo \"⚠ [#1941] Worktree fork base diverged from orchestrator HEAD — auto-degrading to sequential mode for this quick task to avoid a base-mismatch halt.\" >&2\n    USE_WORKTREES=false\n    ISOLATION=none\n  fi\nfi\n\n# Re-resolve (and, as a side effect, re-persist) now that the base-check\n# auto-degrade above may have changed $ISOLATION since the Step 2 gate's\n# `dispatch-isolation` call (#3045). That first call recorded the NATURALLY\n# resolved mode into the run-scoped sentinel the isolation guard hooks read\n# (hooks/gsd-agent-isolation-guard.js, hooks/gsd-cursor-subagent-start.js via\n# hooks/lib/isolation-sentinel.js). The degrade above is decided HERE, in\n# shell — the resolver cannot see it — so without this the sentinel still\n# asserts `harness-worktree` while the dispatch below correctly omits the\n# harness flag, and the guard denies the dispatch with exit 2. `--force-isolation`\n# pushes the FINAL, shell-computed value through that SAME single write path\n# (`none` also clears the stored harnessFlag, since none applies to sequential\n# dispatch). Best-effort: a write failure here must never fail the task — the\n# guards' own sentinel-absent fallback is safe, just less precise.\ngsd_run query dispatch-isolation --raw --force-isolation \"$ISOLATION\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true\n```\n\nCapture current HEAD before spawning (used for worktree branch check):\n```bash\nEXPECTED_BASE=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nif [ \"$ISOLATION\" = \"harness-worktree\" ]; then   # keyed on ISOLATION like every other dispatch-coupled branch (#2652)\n  # BSD/macOS mktemp only randomizes XXXXXX when it is the final path component, so make a\n  # suffixless temp then append the extension — portable across BSD + GNU (#1520).\n  QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST=$(mktemp \"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/gsd-quick-worktree-XXXXXX\") && mv \"$QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\" \"${QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST}.json\" && QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST=\"${QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST}.json\" || exit 1\n  printf '{\"worktrees\":[]}\\n' > \"$QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\"\n  export QUICK_WORKTREE_MANIFEST\nfi\n```\n\nSpawn gsd-executor with plan reference:\n\n```\nAgent(\n  prompt=\"\nExecute quick task ${quick_id}.\n\n${ISOLATION === \"harness-worktree\" ? `\n<worktree_branch_check>\nORCHESTRATOR build-time embed (NOT a sub-agent runtime step): before this dispatch, read \\`gsd-core/references/worktree-branch-check.md\\`, substitute \\`{EXPECTED_BASE}\\` with the base SHA captured above (${EXPECTED_BASE}), substitute \\`{EXPECTED_BASE_ALTERNATE}\\` with \\`${QUICK_PLAN_PARENT}\\` when it differs from \\`${EXPECTED_BASE}\\` (otherwise empty), and replace this note with that fragment's \\`<worktree_branch_check>\\` block so the dispatched prompt carries the runnable guard verbatim — do not pass this instruction through in its place.\n</worktree_branch_check>\n\nFIRST ACTION after the worktree branch check: ensure the quick PLAN.md exists at a worktree-rooted relative path before any Read/Edit/Write path can be primed. If \\`${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md\\` is absent, materialize it from the shared git object store:\n\n\\`\\`\\`bash\nQUICK_PLAN_COMMIT=\"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}\"\nQUICK_PLAN_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md\"\nif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\" ]; then\n  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\")\"\n  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:${QUICK_PLAN_PATH}\" > \"$QUICK_PLAN_PATH\" || {\n    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick plan from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:${QUICK_PLAN_PATH}; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n    exit 42\n  }\nfi\n${DISCUSS_MODE ? `QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-CONTEXT.md\"\nif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" ] && git cat-file -e \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" 2>/dev/null; then\n  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\")\"\n  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" > \"$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH\" || {\n    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick context from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_CONTEXT_PATH; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n    exit 42\n  }\nfi\n` : ''}${RESEARCH_MODE ? `QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH=\"${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-RESEARCH.md\"\nif [ ! -f \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" ] && git cat-file -e \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" 2>/dev/null; then\n  mkdir -p \"$(dirname \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\")\"\n  git show \"${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" > \"$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH\" || {\n    echo \"FATAL: unable to materialize quick research from ${QUICK_PLAN_COMMIT}:$QUICK_RESEARCH_PATH; refusing to continue.\" >&2\n    exit 42\n  }\nfi\n` : ''}\\`\\`\\`\n` : ''}\n\n<required_reading>\n- ${QUICK_DIR}/${quick_id}-PLAN.md (Plan)\n${DISCUSS_MODE ? That is acceptable for a portfolio, but it is the marginal price of the \"both libraries\" decision, so use react-spring where it earns it.\n91\t- Full `motion` component is the heavy part (about 53 kB with presence/spring/gesture features). motion.dev's own figures: `motion` about 34 kB, `m` + `LazyMotion` about 4.6 kB initial (+15 kB `domAnimation`, +25 kB `domMax`), `useAnimate` mini about 2.3 kB. **Optional optimization (not tested):** because motion-primitives is copy-pasted source, you could swap `motion.x` for `m.x` and wrap the app in `LazyMotion`. `AnimatePresence`/`layout`/`drag` need `domMax`, which erodes the saving. Do this only if Lighthouse demands it.\n92\t- Code-split below-the-fold sections with `React.lazy` so the hero ships alone. Any 3D must be lazy-loaded (see Gaps).\n93\t- Not measured: runtime frame rate on a real phone. That belongs in an early performance spike.\n94\t\n95\t## The Four Named Tools\n96\t\n97\t### 1. motion-primitives (ibelick) : USE, as a copy-in source library. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH\n98\t\n99\t- Only deps are `motion`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `lucide-react`, plus a `@/lib/utils` `cn()` helper. **No shadcn init required.**\n100\t- Components are served as shadcn-registry JSON at `https://motion-primitives.com/c/<name>.json` (HTTP 200 verified).\n101\t- The official CLI is `npx motion-primitives@latest add <name>`. It works (tested) but the npm package is stale (0.1.0, March 2025) and **drops files into `./components/motion-primitives/` at the project root, not `src/`**. Either move them to `src/components/motion-primitives/` or skip the CLI and copy the registry JSON content. Decide once and document it.\n102\t- Needs the `@` alias in both `vite.config.ts` (`resolve.alias`) and `tsconfig.app.json` (`paths: { \"@/*\": [\"./src/*\"] }`). With TS 6/7, `paths` works **without** `baseUrl` (verified in the probe). shadcn's Vite doc still says to add `baseUrl`. You do not need it.\n103\t\n104\t### 2. react-spring : USE, scoped. Confidence: HIGH\n105\t\n106\t- Install `@react-spring/web@~10.1.2`. Import `useSpring, useSprings, useTrail, useChain, useTransition, animated, config` from `@react-spring/web`.\n107\t- Version note: `react-spring` (the umbrella, 10.0.4) is behind and v11.0.0-beta (beta.1 released 2026-10-01) is **removing it**, going ESM-only, and deprecating `useTransition` in favor of new `usePresence`/`usePresenceList`. Stay on 10.1.x stable. Avoid `useTransition` for new core UI (use motion's `AnimatePresence`) so the v11 migration stays trivial.\n108\t- Reduced motion is built in: `useReducedMotion` is exported from `@react-spring/web` (confirmed in the type definitions), and the global flag `Globals.assign({ skipAnimation: true })` snaps every spring to its target. Wire it once (snippet below).\n109\t- Dependencies are tiny and tree-shakable. Measured at about +17 kB gz for `useSpring/useTrail/useSprings`.\n110\t\n111\t### 3. realtimecolors.com : USE as a design tool, with caveats. Confidence: HIGH\n112\t\n113\t- The seed (`050315 / fbfbfe / 2f27ce / dedcff / 433bff`, Inter/Inter) is Text, Background, Primary, Secondary, Accent. Note `050315` on `fbfbfe` is the **light** theme pairing (dark text on light bg). The tool's light/dark toggle (Alt+T) lets you design both modes in one session. The project requires light and dark, so design both explicitly rather than inverting.\n114\t- It shows AA/AAA contrast traffic lights per role. Use them as the accessibility gate for text and interactive colors.\n115\t- Exports: CSS variables, Tailwind CSS, SCSS, and custom templates, in HEX/RGB/HSL/OKLAB/OKLCH. The tool lists a `tailwind.config.js` style Tailwind export and does not mention v4. **Export \"CSS\" with OKLCH and paste into `@theme`**, not the Tailwind-config export:\n116\t- Its type-scale presets (1.067 to 1.618) map to a `--text-*` ladder in `@theme`.\n117\t- The user wants alternatives explored: do that in the design phase (Realtime Colors' scheme generators: analogous/complementary/triadic, plus randomize with Space). Keep exactly 5 role tokens plus derived tints so swapping palettes later is a single-block edit.\n118\t\n119\t### 4. godly.design/posts : USE as inspiration only, not a dependency. Confidence: MEDIUM\n120\t\n121\t- `godly.design/posts` is a curated **feed of linked social posts** (mostly X links to designers' UI interaction clips), with category nav (Websites, Tools, Templates, etc.). No dates, tags, RSS, or API were visible. Treat it as a mood-board source: capture screenshots/links into the planning docs, then extract motion patterns (spring feel, press states, cursor effects) into a written reference list before coding. (Probe also noted `godly.website` currently 301-redirects to `recent.design`, so the brand's structure may have shifted. Rely on `godly.design/posts/` and verify when you actually browse.)\n122\t- No code or package implications.\n123\t\n124\t## Division of Responsibility (motion vs react-spring)\n125\t\n126\t| Concern | Owner | Why |\n127\t|---------|-------|-----|\n128\t| Mount/unmount, `AnimatePresence`, exit animations | **motion** | Mature, handles React 19 strict mode and transitions (13.1.1 and 13.4.6 fixes). |\n129\t| Layout and shared-element transitions (`layout`, `layoutId`, Morphing Dialog) | **motion** | react-spring has no equivalent. |\n130\t| Scroll-linked values (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, ScrollProgress, in-view reveals) | **motion** | Already bundled. 13.4.x moved offsets onto hardware-accelerated `ViewTimeline` where supported. |\n131\t| Text/number effects, stagger reveals, all motion-primitives prefabs | **motion** | They are written against it. |\n132\t| Simple drag, `whileTap`, `whileHover` | **motion** | Built in. No extra gesture lib. |\n133\t| Velocity-carrying physics: flick/toss, drag-release with momentum, rubber-band, pointer-chasing follower, squish-on-press toys | **react-spring** | `useSpring` with continuous retargeting is its sweet spot and gives the \"tactile\" signature. |\n134\t| Staggered chains of springs (`useTrail`, `useChain`) for playful sets of elements | **react-spring** | Concise API. |\n135\t| Anything in `@react-spring/three` | **react-spring** | Only if 3D is adopted. |\n136\t\n137\t## Tailwind v4 Notes\n138\t\n139\t- v4 `hover:` only applies under `@media (hover: hover)`, so hover styles never stick after a tap on phones. This matches the \"no desktop-only gestures\" requirement. Do **not** restore v3 behavior with `@custom-variant hover (&:hover)`.\n140\t- Browser floor: Safari 16.4+, Chrome 111+, Firefox 128+ (uses `@property`, `color-mix()`). Fine for this audience.\n141\t- No `tailwind.config.js`; `tw-animate-css` (the shadcn replacement for tailwindcss-animate) is not needed since motion handles animation.\n142\t\n143\t## Dark Mode (covered: Tailwind v4 + tiny script)\n144\t\n145\t## Font Loading\n146\t\n147\t- `import '@fontsource-variable/inter/wght.css'` in `main.tsx`. Verified: it declares `font-display: swap`, `font-weight: 100 900`, and per-script `unicode-range`, so a browser downloads only the **latin subset (48 kB woff2)** for English copy (latin-ext is 85 kB and only fetched if used). Vite emits all subsets to `dist/` (harmless unused files).\n148\t- `@fontsource-variable/inter/opsz.css` adds optical sizing (latin 73 kB). Worth it only if you want display-size tuning.\n149\t- **Preload is not automatic**: a bare `<link rel=\"preload\" href=\"@fontsource-variable/inter/files/...\">` in `index.html` is *not* rewritten by Vite (verified, stays unresolved). If you want a preload, use a small `transformIndexHtml` plugin or `import url from '...woff2?url'`. Skipping preload is acceptable with `font-display: swap`; revisit if Lighthouse flags it.\n150\t- If the design adds a display face from Realtime Colors, use the same Fontsource route (one variable family, latin only), and keep to 2 families total for mobile performance.\n151\t\n152\t## GitHub Pages Deploy\n153\t\n154\t## Alternatives Considered\n155\t\n156\t| Category | Recommended | Alternative | Why Not |\n157\t|----------|-------------|-------------|---------|\n158\t| Framework | Vite + React | Next.js / Astro | Next explicitly out of scope. Astro would be lighter on JS but the user wants React islands everywhere and these two React animation libs. |\n159\t| Animation split | motion for UI choreography + react-spring for signature physics | react-spring only | Loses `layoutId`/`AnimatePresence`/scroll and every motion-primitives component. |\n160\t| Animation split | (same) | motion only | Viable and smaller (saves 17 kB), but the user explicitly wants to try react-spring. Revisit if the spike shows react-spring adds nothing. |\n161\t| react-spring package | `@react-spring/web` | `react-spring` umbrella | Lagging, `deprecated` tag present, removed in v11 beta. |\n162\t| Installing primitives | Copy via registry JSON / CLI, then patch | Wait for an npm package | None exists. Source ownership is the model. |\n163\t| Styling | Tailwind v4 | Tailwind v3.4 | Only for pre-2023 browsers. motion-primitives and shadcn are on v4. |\n164\t| TypeScript | 6.0.x | 7.0.x | 7.0.2 compiled the probe cleanly, so it is available, but v6 is the scaffold default. Take 7 post-launch. |\n165\t| Dark mode | Tailwind data-attr + inline script | next-themes | Next-oriented abstraction we do not need. |\n166\t| Fonts | Fontsource self-hosted | Google Fonts `<link>` | Third-party request, render-blocking, privacy. |\n167\t\n168\t## What NOT to Use\n169\t\n170\t| Do not use | Why |\n171\t|------------|-----|\n172\t| `react-spring` umbrella package | Deprecated tag, removed in v11. Use `@react-spring/web`. |\n173\t| `react-spring` v11 beta | Beta, breaking `onRest`/`immediate` semantics. Wait for stable. |\n174\t| Raw `framer-motion` imports | motion 14 depends on `framer-motion` internally. Always import from `motion/react` so there is one copy. |\n175\t| motion-primitives `Cursor`, `Magnetic`, `Tilt`, `Spotlight` as *primary* interactions | Mouse-only. They are desktop garnish. |\n176\t| `shadcn init` | Unneeded; adds Radix/CVA you will not use. |\n177\t| `tailwind.config.js`, `@tailwind base/components/utilities` | v3 patterns. |\n178\t| `@use-gesture/react` by default | Last published March 2024, and motion already provides drag/tap/hover. Last publish Oct 2025. |\n180\t| Hard-coded absolute asset paths | Break when `base` changes. |\n181\t| Both libraries animating the same element/property | The one real coexistence hazard. |\n182\t\n183\t## Gaps: capabilities the four tools do NOT cover\n184\t\n185\t| Gap | Already covered? | Candidate A (default) | Candidate B | Notes |\n186\t|-----|------------------|-----------------------|-------------|-------|\n187\t| **Scroll-driven animation** | **Yes, by `motion`** (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, `scroll()`, motion-primitives ScrollProgress/InView) | motion `useScroll` (+0 kB) | GSAP ScrollTrigger (+44 kB gz measured). Smooth scroll is a taste item, not a requirement. A visitor who starts with reduced motion on and turns it off mid-visit never receives the motion feature bundle, so every `m` consumer (ToastPresence, PillMorph) stays static for the rest of the visit.\n\nRoot cause: `src/anim/MotionFeatures.tsx` chooses the LazyMotion loader once, from `reduced` at mount (`never` when reduced motion is on). LazyMotion calls its `features` function once, at mount, and never again. The later `[reduced]` effect only warms the chunk through a dynamic import, which LazyMotion never consumes, so `markFeaturesLoaded` is never called after the flip.\n\nFix: hold the loader on a per-mount deferred gate. The gate stays pending while reduced motion is on, and the `[reduced]` effect releases it when the preference flips off. If `open` is true, call the resolver immediately. `loader` is a zero-argument function that returns the gate promise chained with `.then(loadFeatures)`. `release` calls the resolver, and calling it more than once must be harmless.\n\n2. Calling `release` more than once is harmless.\n */\nfunction createFeatureGate(open: boolean): FeatureGate {\n  let resolve: () => void = () => {}\n  const ready = new Promise<void>((r) => {\n    resolve = () => r()\n  })\n  if (open) resolve()\n  return {\n    loader: () => ready.then(loadFeatures),\n    release: () => resolve(),\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * The one LazyMotion boundary.
